```cpp
        q.questions.push_back({randomizeCase(qname), qtype, rrclass::IN});
        q.edns = Edns{};
        auto wire = serializeMessage(q);

        auto raw = exchangeUdp(server, wire, q.header.id, kTimeoutMs);
        for (int attempt = 0; attempt < 2 && raw; ++attempt) {
            try {
                Message r = parseMessage(*raw);
                if (!r.header.qr || r.header.id != q.header.id || r.questions.size() != 1 ||
                    !exactSameName(r.questions[0].qname, q.questions[0].qname) ||
                    r.questions[0].qtype != qtype || r.questions[0].qclass != rrclass::IN)
                    return std::nullopt;
                if (r.header.tc && attempt == 0) {  // RFC 7766 §5: retry the same query over TCP
                    raw = exchangeTcp(server, wire, kTimeoutMs);
                    continue;
                }
                return r;
            } catch (const ParseError&) {
                return std::nullopt;
            }
        }
        return std::nullopt;
    }

    // The deepest zone for which we know name servers, from the cache, else the root.
    // DS lives in the parent zone, so a DS search starts one label up (RFC 4035 §3.1.4.1).
    std::pair<DnsName, std::vector<DnsName>> closestDelegation(const DnsName& qname, uint16_t qtype) {
        for (DnsName n = (qtype == rrtype::DS && !qname.isRoot()) ? qname.parent() : qname;; n = n.parent()) {
            if (auto e = cache_.get(n, rrtype::NS, Trust::Glue); e && !e->negative) {
                std::vector<DnsName> names;
                for (const auto& rr : e->records) names.push_back(DnsName::fromWire(rr.rdata));
                return {n, names};
            }
            if (n.isRoot()) return {n, {}};
        }
    }

    std::vector<sockaddr_in> addressesOf(const std::vector<DnsName>& nsNames, const DnsName& zone, int depth) {
        std::vector<sockaddr_in> out;
        auto collect = [&](const std::vector<ResourceRecord>& rrs) {
            for (const auto& rr : rrs)
                if (rr.type == rrtype::A && rr.rdata.size() == 4) {
                    sockaddr_in a{};
                    a.sin_family = AF_INET;
                    a.sin_port = htons(53);
                    std::memcpy(&a.sin_addr, rr.rdata.data(), 4);
                    out.push_back(a);
                }
        };
        for (const auto& ns : nsNames)
            if (auto e = cache_.get(ns, rrtype::A, Trust::Glue); e && !e->negative) collect(e->records);
        if (out.empty())
            for (const auto& ns : nsNames) {
                if (ns.isSubdomainOf(zone)) continue;  // needs glue we do not have: would loop
                Resolution r = resolve(ns, rrtype::A, depth + 1);
                collect(r.answers);
                if (!out.empty()) break;
            }
        // Spread load; a production resolver orders by smoothed RTT instead.
        for (size_t i = out.size(); i > 1; --i) std::swap(out[i - 1], out[random16() % i]);
        return out;
    }

    static std::vector<ResourceRecord> select(const std::vector<ResourceRecord>& rrs, const DnsName& name, uint16_t type) {
        std::vector<ResourceRecord> out;
        for (const auto& rr : rrs)
            if (rr.type == type && rr.name == name) out.push_back(rr);
        return out;
    }

    struct Step {
        enum Kind { Final, Cname } kind = Final;
        std::vector<ResourceRecord> records;  // CNAMEs followed inside this response + final RRset
        uint16_t rcode = rcode::NOERROR;
        std::optional<ResourceRecord> soa;
        DnsName next;  // for Cname: where the chain continues
    };

    // Follows referrals from the closest known delegation until an authoritative server
    // answers. Every result is also written to the cache. nullopt means SERVFAIL.
    std::optional<Step> iterate(const DnsName& qname, uint16_t qtype, int depth) {
        auto [zone, nsNames] = closestDelegation(qname, qtype);
        for (int referrals = 0; referrals < kMaxReferrals; ++referrals) {
            auto servers = nsNames.empty() ? rootHints() : addressesOf(nsNames, zone, depth);
            std::optional<Message> resp;
            for (const auto& s : servers) {
                resp = query(s, qname, qtype);
                if (resp && (resp->rcode() == rcode::NOERROR || resp->rcode() == rcode::NXDOMAIN)) break;
                resp.reset();  // timeout, SERVFAIL, REFUSED, garbage: try the next server
            }
            if (!resp) return std::nullopt;

            // Bailiwick rule: a server for `zone` may only tell us about names inside `zone`.
            auto outside = [&](const ResourceRecord& rr) { return !rr.name.isSubdomainOf(zone); };
            std::erase_if(resp->answers, outside);
            std::erase_if(resp->authority, outside);
            std::erase_if(resp->additional, outside);

            // Follow any CNAME chain contained in this answer section.
            Step step;
            DnsName name = qname;
            for (int i = 0; i < kMaxCnameHops; ++i) {
                auto cn = select(resp->answers, name, rrtype::CNAME);
                if (cn.empty() || qtype == rrtype::CNAME) break;
                cache_.putRRset(cn, Trust::Answer);
                step.records.push_back(cn[0]);
                name = DnsName::fromWire(cn[0].rdata);
            }

            auto soa = std::find_if(resp->authority.begin(), resp->authority.end(),
                                    [](const auto& rr) { return rr.type == rrtype::SOA; });
            if (soa != resp->authority.end()) step.soa = *soa;

            if (resp->rcode() == rcode::NXDOMAIN) {  // RFC 6604: refers to the last name in the chain
                step.rcode = rcode::NXDOMAIN;
                if (step.soa) cache_.putNegative(name, qtype, rcode::NXDOMAIN, *step.soa);  // RFC 2308 §5
                return step;
            }
            if (auto ans = select(resp->answers, name, qtype); !ans.empty()) {
                cache_.putRRset(ans, Trust::Answer);
                step.records.insert(step.records.end(), ans.begin(), ans.end());
                return step;
            }
            if (!step.records.empty()) {  // chain continues outside what this server told us
                step.kind = Step::Cname;
                step.next = name;
                return step;
            }

            // Referral: NS set for a zone strictly below the current one that contains qname.
            std::optional<DnsName> child;
            std::vector<ResourceRecord> nsSet;
            for (const auto& rr : resp->authority)
                if (rr.type == rrtype::NS && qname.isSubdomainOf(rr.name) && rr.name.labelCount() > zone.labelCount() &&
                    !(qtype == rrtype::DS && rr.name == qname)) {  // never descend into the child for DS
                    if (!child) child = rr.name;
                    if (rr.name == *child) nsSet.push_back(rr);
                }
            if (child && !resp->header.aa) {
                cache_.putRRset(nsSet, Trust::Glue);
                std::vector<DnsName> names;
                for (const auto& rr : nsSet) names.push_back(DnsName::fromWire(rr.rdata));
                for (const auto& n : names)
                    if (auto glue = select(resp->additional, n, rrtype::A); !glue.empty())
                        cache_.putRRset(glue, Trust::Glue);
                zone = *child;
                nsNames = std::move(names);
                continue;
            }

            // NODATA: NOERROR, nothing for qname/qtype, SOA in authority (RFC 2308 §2.2).
            if (step.soa) {
                cache_.putNegative(qname, qtype, rcode::NOERROR, *step.soa);
                return step;
            }
            return std::nullopt;  // lame or nonsensical response
        }
        return std::nullopt;
    }

    Cache cache_;
};

}  // namespace dns
```

**Data structures:**

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| `Cache::Entry` | one RRset or negative answer, plus absolute expiry and `Trust` rank |
| `Cache` | hash map keyed by `name.key() + type`; type 0 = "NXDOMAIN for every type" |
| `Resolution` | what the server returns: RCODE, answer chain, SOA for negatives |
| `Resolver::Step` | the outcome of one `iterate()`: final answer/negative, or CNAME continuation |

**Test** (needs internet access; the results are from a real run on 2026-10-20):

```bash
./dnsd -p 5353 -z example.com. example.com.zone -r &
dig @127.0.0.1 -p 5353 www.wikipedia.org A      # CNAME dyna.wikimedia.org + A; 0.32 s cold
dig @127.0.0.1 -p 5353 www.wikipedia.org A      # same, 0.00 s, TTLs counted down by 1 s: cache
dig @127.0.0.1 -p 5353 does-not-exist-7f3a.iana.org   # NXDOMAIN + iana.org SOA
dig @127.0.0.1 -p 5353 ietf.org DS              # answered by the .org servers (parent side)
dig @127.0.0.1 -p 5353 www.example.com          # still answered authoritatively from our zone
```

### 17.11 Stage 10 — hardening, and the complete server

`src/server.cpp` ties everything together. It runs a single-threaded `poll()` loop over the UDP socket, the TCP listener and the TCP clients. It decides the opcode from the header alone (NOTIMP), never answers responses, recurses only for loopback clients, limits TCP connections and buffers, and resets the idle timer only on complete messages.

```cpp
// Tutorial DNS server: authoritative for the zones given on the command line, and optionally
// an iterative resolver for loopback clients.
//
//   ./dnsd -p 5353 -z example.com. example.com.zone [-z <origin> <file>]... [-r]

#include <dns/authority.hpp>
#include <dns/resolver.hpp>

#include <fcntl.h>
#include <netinet/in.h>
#include <poll.h>
#include <sys/socket.h>
#include <unistd.h>

#include <cerrno>
#include <cstdio>
#include <cstring>
#include <ctime>
#include <fstream>
#include <map>
#include <sstream>

using namespace dns;

namespace {

constexpr size_t kMaxTcpClients = 64;
constexpr time_t kTcpIdleSeconds = 10;  // RFC 7766 §6.2.3: "on the order of seconds"
constexpr size_t kMaxTcpBacklog = 1 << 20;  // unsent response bytes per connection

struct Config {
    uint16_t port = 5353;
    bool recursion = false;
};

struct TcpClient {
    std::vector<uint8_t> in, out;
    time_t lastActive = 0;
};

AuthoritativeServer g_auth;
Resolver g_resolver;
Config g_cfg;

int fail(const char* what) {
    std::perror(what);
    return 1;
}

void setNonBlocking(int fd) { fcntl(fd, F_SETFL, fcntl(fd, F_GETFL) | O_NONBLOCK); }

bool isLoopback(const sockaddr_in& a) { return (ntohl(a.sin_addr.s_addr) >> 24) == 127; }

// Header-only error reply, built from the raw header alone: used when the body cannot be
// (or, for unknown opcodes, need not be) parsed (RFC 1035 §4.1.1, RFC 8906 §7).
std::vector<uint8_t> errorReply(std::span<const uint8_t> query, uint8_t rc) {
    Message r;
    r.header.id = uint16_t(query[0] << 8 | query[1]);
    r.header.qr = true;
    r.header.opcode = (query[2] >> 3) & 0xF;
    r.header.rd = query[2] & 0x01;
    r.header.rcode = rc;
    return serializeMessage(r);
}

Message recursiveAnswer(const Message& q) {
    Message r;
    r.header.id = q.header.id;
    r.header.qr = r.header.rd = r.header.ra = true;
    r.header.cd = q.header.cd;  // RFC 4035 §3.2.2: a recursive server copies CD
    r.questions = q.questions;
    if (q.edns) r.edns = Edns{};
    if (q.header.opcode != opcode::QUERY || q.questions.size() != 1 || q.questions[0].qclass != rrclass::IN) {
        r.setRcode(q.questions.size() != 1 ? rcode::FORMERR : rcode::NOTIMP);
        return r;
    }
    Resolution res = g_resolver.resolve(q.questions[0].qname, q.questions[0].qtype);
    r.setRcode(res.rcode);
    r.answers = std::move(res.answers);
    if (res.soa) r.authority.push_back(*res.soa);
    return r;
}

// Turns one request into one response; nullopt means "send nothing".
std::optional<std::vector<uint8_t>> handle(std::span<const uint8_t> wire, const sockaddr_in& from, bool tcp) {
    if (wire.size() < 12) return std::nullopt;  // not even a header: drop silently
    if (wire[2] & 0x80) return std::nullopt;    // QR=1: never answer responses (reflection loops)
    if (((wire[2] >> 3) & 0xF) != opcode::QUERY) return errorReply(wire, rcode::NOTIMP);  // RFC 8906 §7
    Message q;
    try {
        q = parseMessage(wire);
    } catch (const ParseError&) {
        return errorReply(wire, rcode::FORMERR);
    }
    bool mayRecurse = g_cfg.recursion && isLoopback(from);  // never run an open resolver
    Message r = g_auth.answer(q);
    if (mayRecurse && q.header.rd && r.rcode() == rcode::REFUSED && q.questions.size() == 1)
        r = recursiveAnswer(q);  // not one of our zones
    r.header.ra = mayRecurse;

    // UDP size limit: 512 without EDNS (RFC 1035 §4.2.1); with EDNS the smaller of the
    // client's and our own size, never below 512 (RFC 6891 §6.2.3, §6.2.5).
    size_t limit = 65535;
    if (!tcp) limit = q.edns ? std::clamp<size_t>(q.edns->udpSize, 512, AuthoritativeServer::kOurUdpSize) : 512;
    return serializeMessage(r, limit);
}

int openUdp(uint16_t port) {
    int fd = socket(AF_INET, SOCK_DGRAM, 0);
    if (fd < 0) return -1;
    sockaddr_in a{};
    a.sin_family = AF_INET;
    a.sin_port = htons(port);
    a.sin_addr.s_addr = htonl(INADDR_ANY);
    if (bind(fd, reinterpret_cast<sockaddr*>(&a), sizeof a) < 0) return -1;
    setNonBlocking(fd);
    return fd;
}

int openTcp(uint16_t port) {
    int fd = socket(AF_INET, SOCK_STREAM, 0);
    if (fd < 0) return -1;
    int one = 1;
    setsockopt(fd, SOL_SOCKET, SO_REUSEADDR, &one, sizeof one);
    sockaddr_in a{};
    a.sin_family = AF_INET;
    a.sin_port = htons(port);
    a.sin_addr.s_addr = htonl(INADDR_ANY);
    if (bind(fd, reinterpret_cast<sockaddr*>(&a), sizeof a) < 0 || listen(fd, 64) < 0) return -1;
    setNonBlocking(fd);
    return fd;
}

void serveUdp(int fd) {
    uint8_t buf[65535];
    for (;;) {
        sockaddr_in from{};
        socklen_t fromLen = sizeof from;
        ssize_t n = recvfrom(fd, buf, sizeof buf, 0, reinterpret_cast<sockaddr*>(&from), &fromLen);
        if (n < 0) return;  // EAGAIN: drained
        if (auto resp = handle({buf, size_t(n)}, from, false))
            sendto(fd, resp->data(), resp->size(), 0, reinterpret_cast<sockaddr*>(&from), fromLen);
    }
}

// Extracts every complete length-prefixed message from the client's input buffer
// (RFC 7766 §6.2.1: clients may pipeline several queries on one connection).
void processTcpInput(int fd, TcpClient& c) {
    sockaddr_in peer{};
    socklen_t len = sizeof peer;
    getpeername(fd, reinterpret_cast<sockaddr*>(&peer), &len);
    while (c.in.size() >= 2) {
        size_t msgLen = size_t(c.in[0] << 8 | c.in[1]);
        if (c.in.size() < 2 + msgLen) break;
        std::span<const uint8_t> msg(c.in.data() + 2, msgLen);
        c.lastActive = time(nullptr);  // only complete messages count (RFC 7766 §6.2.3 slow-read)
        if (auto resp = handle(msg, peer, true)) {
            c.out.push_back(uint8_t(resp->size() >> 8));  // length prefix and message in one buffer:
            c.out.push_back(uint8_t(resp->size()));       // one write, no Nagle stall (RFC 7766 §8)
            c.out.insert(c.out.end(), resp->begin(), resp->end());
        }
        c.in.erase(c.in.begin(), c.in.begin() + long(2 + msgLen));
    }
}

int run(int udp, int tcp) {
    std::map<int, TcpClient> clients;
    for (;;) {
        std::vector<pollfd> fds{{udp, POLLIN, 0}, {tcp, POLLIN, 0}};
        for (auto& [fd, c] : clients) fds.push_back({fd, short(POLLIN | (c.out.empty() ? 0 : POLLOUT)), 0});
        if (poll(fds.data(), fds.size(), 1000) < 0 && errno != EINTR) return fail("poll");

        if (fds[0].revents & POLLIN) serveUdp(udp);
        if (fds[1].revents & POLLIN) {
            int c = accept(tcp, nullptr, nullptr);
            if (c >= 0) {
                if (clients.size() >= kMaxTcpClients) {
                    close(c);  // shed load instead of exhausting file descriptors
                } else {
                    setNonBlocking(c);
                    clients[c].lastActive = time(nullptr);
                }
            }
        }
        time_t now = time(nullptr);
        for (size_t i = 2; i < fds.size(); ++i) {
            int fd = fds[i].fd;
            TcpClient& c = clients[fd];
            bool dead = fds[i].revents & (POLLERR | POLLHUP | POLLNVAL);
            if (!dead && (fds[i].revents & POLLIN)) {
                uint8_t buf[4096];
                ssize_t n = recv(fd, buf, sizeof buf, 0);
                if (n <= 0) dead = true;
                else {
                    c.in.insert(c.in.end(), buf, buf + n);
                    processTcpInput(fd, c);
                    if (c.out.size() > kMaxTcpBacklog) dead = true;  // client sends but never reads
                }
            }
            if (!dead && !c.out.empty()) {
                ssize_t n = send(fd, c.out.data(), c.out.size(), MSG_NOSIGNAL);
                if (n > 0) c.out.erase(c.out.begin(), c.out.begin() + n);
                else if (n < 0 && errno != EAGAIN) dead = true;
            }
            if (dead || now - c.lastActive > kTcpIdleSeconds) {
                close(fd);
                clients.erase(fd);
            }
        }
    }
}

}  // namespace

int main(int argc, char** argv) {
    std::setvbuf(stdout, nullptr, _IOLBF, 0);  // line-buffered log output even when redirected
    try {
        for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {
            std::string a = argv[i];
            if (a == "-p" && i + 1 < argc) {
                g_cfg.port = uint16_t(std::stoi(argv[++i]));
            } else if (a == "-r") {
                g_cfg.recursion = true;
            } else if (a == "-z" && i + 2 < argc) {
                DnsName origin = DnsName::fromText(argv[++i]);
                std::ifstream f(argv[++i]);
                if (!f) throw std::runtime_error(std::string("cannot open ") + argv[i]);
                std::stringstream ss;
                ss << f.rdbuf();
                g_auth.addZone(parseZone(ss.str(), origin));
                std::printf("loaded zone %s\n", origin.toText().c_str());
            } else {
                std::fprintf(stderr, "usage: %s [-p port] [-z origin file]... [-r]\n", argv[0]);
                return 2;
            }
        }
    } catch (const std::exception& e) {
        std::fprintf(stderr, "error: %s\n", e.what());
        return 1;
    }

    int udp = openUdp(g_cfg.port), tcp = openTcp(g_cfg.port);
    if (udp < 0 || tcp < 0) return fail("socket/bind");
    std::printf("listening on port %u (udp+tcp)%s\n", g_cfg.port, g_cfg.recursion ? ", recursion for loopback" : "");
    return run(udp, tcp);
}
```

**Hardening already in place**, with section references:

| Measure | Where |
|---|---|
| Bounds-checked parsing, loop-proof decompression, 255/63 limits | `WireReader`, `DnsName::read` (§5.6) |
| No answers to QR=1; header-only NOTIMP/FORMERR | `handle()`, `errorReply()` (§7.3) |
| UDP size never above the client's advertised size or 1232 | `handle()` (§9.4) |
| No open resolver (loopback only) | `isLoopback()` (§16.4) |
| TCP: connection cap 64, idle 10 s reset on full messages only, 1 MiB output cap | `run()`, `processTcpInput()` (§8.5) |
| Resolver: CSPRNG IDs, fresh socket per query (random port), `connect()`ed UDP, 0x20, exact question match | `resolver.hpp` (§16.2–16.3) |
| Resolver: bailiwick filter, trust ranks, limits on depth, referrals and CNAME hops | `iterate()`, `Cache` (§12.4, §12.6) |

**Still missing for production**, in rough order of importance:

1. **Response Rate Limiting and DNS Cookies** on the authoritative side (§16.4).
2. **Concurrency.** The resolver blocks the whole loop while it resolves. Make outgoing queries asynchronous (one state machine per client query), or use a thread pool, with locks on the cache.
3. **IPv6**: listen on `AF_INET6` with `IPV6_V6ONLY=0` or on separate sockets; add AAAA glue and IPv6 root hints. Bind per address, or use `IP_PKTINFO`, so replies come from the address that was queried (§8.2).
4. **Resolution-failure cache** (RFC 9520), priming (RFC 9609), QNAME minimisation (RFC 9156), RTT-based server selection, NXNS limits per delegation.
5. **DNSSEC**: serving signed zones (RRSIG/NSEC with DO=1, §15.8) and/or validation (§15.9).
6. Privilege dropping after `bind()`, configuration reload, zone reload without dropping queries, logging and metrics.
7. Zone transfers, NOTIFY, UPDATE and TSIG (§14).

### 17.12 Known deviations in the reference code

These are documented simplifications. None of them is a bug for the tested scope.

- When the server hosts both a parent and a child zone, it answers DS from the child, which is wrong (§11.8).
- Zone files: `$INCLUDE` and `$GENERATE` are not supported, and the text parsers cover only A, AAAA, NS, CNAME, PTR, DNAME, MX, SRV, SOA, TXT, HINFO and CAA. Every other type must use the RFC 3597 `\#` form. Mixed classes and missing glue are not detected.
- The resolver does not prime, queries IPv4 servers only, and caches NXDOMAIN per exact name. It does not use the RFC 8020 subtree cut.
- `AuthoritativeServer` answers only class IN, and refuses AXFR/IXFR.

## 18. Testing and Debugging

### 18.1 `dig` (BIND)

`dig` is the reference client. It shows every header flag and section, and it complains clearly about malformed messages.

| Command | What it tests |
|---|---|
| `dig @127.0.0.1 -p 5353 www.example.com A` | basic query to a server on a non-standard port |
| `… +norec` | RD=0, the way resolvers talk to authoritative servers. Avoids "recursion requested but not available". |
| `… +tcp` | force TCP (§8.4) |
| `… +ignore +noedns` | do not retry over TCP on TC=1, so you see the truncated reply (§8.3) |
| `… +bufsize=512` / `+bufsize=1232` | advertised EDNS payload size (§9.4) |
| `… +noedns` | no OPT at all: the 512-byte rules apply |
| `… +edns=1 +noednsneg` | EDNS version 1 without fallback: expect BADVERS (§9.6) |
| `… +dnssec` | sets DO=1; expect RRSIGs from signed zones (§15.7) |
| `… +cd` / `+adflag` | set CD / AD in the query |
| `… +nsid` / `+cookie` | send the NSID / COOKIE options (§9.5) |
| `… +qr` | also print the query `dig` sent |
| `… +noall +answer` / `+short` | output only the answer section / only RDATA |
| `… +multi` | readable multi-line SOA/DNSKEY output, including the key tag of each DNSKEY |
| `… -t AXFR example.com` | zone transfer (§14.1) |
| `dig -x 192.0.2.10` | reverse lookup, i.e. `10.2.0.192.in-addr.arpa PTR` (§3.8) |
| `dig +trace www.example.com` | `dig` iterates from the root by itself; compare with your resolver (§12) |

How to read `dig` warnings:

| Message | Likely cause in your server |
|---|---|
| `;; Warning: Message parser reports malformed message packet.` | wrong counts, a bad compression pointer, RDLENGTH not matching the RDATA, trailing bytes |
| `;; Warning: ID mismatch: expected ID …, got …` | ID not copied, or byte order swapped (§4.2) |
| `;; WARNING: recursion requested but not available` | you sent RD=1 to an authoritative-only server (normal); use `+norec` |
| `;; Truncated, retrying in TCP mode.` | TC=1 was set (expected for big answers), so check TCP works |
| `;; communications error … connection refused` / `timed out` | not listening on that port/protocol, firewall, or the server drops the query (perhaps it thinks QR=1) |
| no `OPT PSEUDOSECTION` in the reply | you did not return OPT for an EDNS query (§9.1) |
| `;; Question section mismatch` | you changed the question (case, type or class) |

### 18.2 `drill` and `kdig`

- **drill** (from ldns): `drill -p 5353 www.example.com @127.0.0.1`. `drill -T example.com` traces from the root. `drill -D -S example.com` chases and checks the DNSSEC signature chain, which is useful for §15.
- **kdig** (from Knot DNS) accepts the same options as `dig` (`+norec`, `+tcp`, `+bufsize`, …) and also speaks encrypted transports: `kdig @1.1.1.1 +tls example.com` (DoT), `kdig @1.1.1.1 +https example.com` (DoH), `kdig @… +quic …` (DoQ, recent versions). It also prints EDE options in readable form.
- **Python, no dependencies:** this is the client that checked every expected result listed in §17, because `dig` was not installed on the test machine. It builds queries by hand (`raw=` sends arbitrary bytes, which is how the FORMERR and NOTIMP cases were tested) and decodes responses, including compression pointers. For valid traffic in larger test suites, the usual Python library is `dnspython`.

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Minimal DNS client for testing the tutorial server: python3 client.py [port] [server]"""
import socket, struct, sys, random

PORT = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 5353
HOST = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else '127.0.0.1'
TYPES = {1:'A',2:'NS',5:'CNAME',6:'SOA',12:'PTR',15:'MX',16:'TXT',28:'AAAA',33:'SRV',39:'DNAME',41:'OPT',257:'CAA',13:'HINFO'}
RCODES = {0:'NOERROR',1:'FORMERR',2:'SERVFAIL',3:'NXDOMAIN',4:'NOTIMP',5:'REFUSED',16:'BADVERS'}

def enc(n):
    out = b''
    for l in n.rstrip('.').split('.'):
        if l: out += bytes([len(l)]) + l.encode()
    return out + b'\0'

def dec(m, p):
    labels, end = [], None
    while True:
        l = m[p]
        if l >= 0xC0:
            if end is None: end = p + 2
            p = ((l & 0x3F) << 8) | m[p+1]; continue
        if l == 0:
            return '.'.join(labels) + '.', (end if end is not None else p + 1)
        labels.append(m[p+1:p+1+l].decode('latin1')); p += 1 + l

def query(name, qtype, rd=0, edns=None, tcp=False, raw=None):
    qid = random.randint(0, 65535)
    if raw is None:
        ar = 1 if edns is not None else 0
        msg = struct.pack('!HHHHHH', qid, rd << 8, 1, 0, 0, ar) + enc(name) + struct.pack('!HH', qtype, 1)
        if edns is not None:
            size, version = edns
            msg += b'\0' + struct.pack('!HHIH', 41, size, version << 16, 0)
    else:
        msg = raw
    if tcp:
        s = socket.create_connection((HOST, PORT), timeout=3)
        s.sendall(struct.pack('!H', len(msg)) + msg)
        ln = struct.unpack('!H', s.recv(2))[0]
        data = b''
        while len(data) < ln: data += s.recv(ln - len(data))
        s.close()
    else:
        s = socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_DGRAM); s.settimeout(3)
        s.sendto(msg, (HOST, PORT))
        data = s.recv(65535)
    return parse(data)

def rdtext(m, t, p, ln):
    rd = m[p:p+ln]
    if t == 1: return socket.inet_ntoa(rd)
    if t == 28: return socket.inet_ntop(socket.AF_INET6, rd)
    if t in (2, 5, 12, 39): return dec(m, p)[0]
    if t == 15: return f'{struct.unpack("!H", rd[:2])[0]} {dec(m, p+2)[0]}'
    if t == 33: return f'{" ".join(map(str, struct.unpack("!HHH", rd[:6])))} {dec(m, p+6)[0]}'
    if t == 6:
        a, q = dec(m, p); b, q = dec(m, q)
        return f'{a} {b} ' + ' '.join(map(str, struct.unpack('!IIIII', m[q:q+20])))
    if t == 16:
        out, i = [], 0
        while i < len(rd): out.append('"' + rd[i+1:i+1+rd[i]].decode() + '"'); i += 1 + rd[i]
        return ' '.join(out)
    return rd.hex()

def parse(m):
    qid, fl, qd, an, ns, ar = struct.unpack('!HHHHHH', m[:12])
    flags = [n for b, n in [(15,'qr'),(10,'aa'),(9,'tc'),(8,'rd'),(7,'ra'),(5,'ad'),(4,'cd')] if fl >> b & 1]
    r = {'rcode': fl & 0xF, 'flags': flags, 'counts': (qd, an, ns, ar), 'sections': [[], [], []], 'size': len(m)}
    p = 12
    for _ in range(qd):
        n, p = dec(m, p); p += 4
    for si, cnt in enumerate((an, ns, ar)):
        for _ in range(cnt):
            n, p = dec(m, p)
            t, c, ttl, ln = struct.unpack('!HHIH', m[p:p+10]); p += 10
            if t == 41:
                r['opt'] = {'size': c, 'ext_rcode': ttl >> 24, 'version': (ttl >> 16) & 0xFF}
                r['rcode'] |= (ttl >> 24) << 4
            else:
                r['sections'][si].append(f'{n} {ttl} {TYPES.get(t, t)} {rdtext(m, t, p, ln)}')
            p += ln
    return r

def show(title, r):
    print(f'--- {title}: {RCODES.get(r["rcode"], r["rcode"])} flags={",".join(r["flags"])} counts={r["counts"]} size={r["size"]}')
    for name, sec in zip(('ANSWER', 'AUTHORITY', 'ADDITIONAL'), r['sections']):
        for rr in sec: print(f'  {name:10} {rr}')
    if 'opt' in r: print('  OPT', r['opt'])

if __name__ == '__main__':
    tests = [
        ('www A', ('www.example.com', 1)),
        ('web A (CNAME)', ('web.example.com', 1)),
        ('nope A (NXDOMAIN)', ('nope.example.com', 1)),
        ('www MX (NODATA)', ('www.example.com', 15)),
        ('x.wild A (wildcard)', ('x.wild.example.com', 1)),
        ('b.c TXT (empty non-terminal)', ('b.c.example.com', 16)),
        ('host.sub A (referral)', ('host.sub.example.com', 1)),
        ('x.legacy A (DNAME)', ('x.legacy.example.com', 1)),
        ('apex MX (additional)', ('example.com', 15)),
        ('SRV', ('_sip._udp.example.com', 33)),
        ('CASE 0x20', ('wWw.ExAmPlE.cOm', 1)),
        ('example.org (REFUSED)', ('example.org', 1)),
    ]
    for title, (n, t) in tests:
        show(title, query(n, t))
    show('www A over TCP', query('www.example.com', 1, tcp=True))
    show('EDNS 1232', query('www.example.com', 1, edns=(1232, 0)))
    show('EDNS version 1 (BADVERS)', query('www.example.com', 1, edns=(1232, 1)))
    show('garbage (FORMERR)', query(None, None, raw=bytes.fromhex('abcd0100000100000000000003777777')))
```

```bash
python3 client.py 5353            # the §17.7 queries plus TCP, EDNS, BADVERS and FORMERR checks
```

### 18.3 Packet capture

```bash
# capture on loopback, port 5353, full packets, to a file
sudo tcpdump -i lo -n -s 0 -w dns.pcap 'port 5353'
# live hex + ASCII view
sudo tcpdump -i lo -n -X 'udp port 5353'
```

**Wireshark** decodes DNS on port 53 automatically. For 5353 it may decode the traffic as mDNS. Use *Analyze → Decode As… → UDP port 5353 → DNS*. Useful display filters:

| Filter | Shows |
|---|---|
| `dns` | all DNS |
| `dns.flags.response == 0` | queries only |
| `dns.flags.rcode == 3` | NXDOMAIN responses |
| `dns.flags.truncated == 1` | TC=1 |
| `dns.qry.name == "www.example.com"` | one name |
| `dns.opt` | messages with EDNS |
| `tcp.port == 5353 && dns` | DNS over TCP (Wireshark reassembles the length-prefixed stream) |

Wireshark marks malformed packets with `[Malformed Packet]` and highlights the offending bytes. This is the fastest way to find serialiser bugs.

### 18.4 Unit tests for the wire layer

This test program checks four things. Parsing the §4.5 query gives the right fields. Building the §4.6 response gives **byte-identical** output. The CNAME, NXDOMAIN, referral and EDNS dumps from §4–§11 round-trip byte for byte. Truncation, and every hostile input in the §5.6 table, are handled.

```cpp
#include <dns/message.hpp>

#include <arpa/inet.h>
#include <cassert>
#include <cstdio>

using namespace dns;

static std::vector<uint8_t> hex(const char* s) {
    std::string digits;
    for (; *s; ++s)
        if (*s != ' ' && *s != '\n') digits.push_back(*s);
    assert(digits.size() % 2 == 0);
    std::vector<uint8_t> v;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < digits.size(); i += 2) v.push_back(uint8_t(std::stoul(digits.substr(i, 2), nullptr, 16)));
    return v;
}

static void expectThrow(std::vector<uint8_t> v, const char* what) {
    try {
        parseMessage(v);
        printf("FAIL: no throw for %s\n", what);
    } catch (const ParseError& e) {
        printf("ok: %s -> %s\n", what, e.what());
    }
}

int main() {
    auto q1 = hex("1a2b0100000100000000000003777777076578616d706c6503636f6d0000010001");
    Message q = parseMessage(q1);
    assert(q.header.id == 0x1a2b && q.header.rd && q.questions.size() == 1);
    assert(q.questions[0].qname.toText() == "www.example.com.");

    Message r;
    r.header.id = q.header.id;
    r.header.qr = r.header.aa = r.header.rd = true;
    r.questions = q.questions;
    ResourceRecord a{q.questions[0].qname, rrtype::A, rrclass::IN, 3600, {192, 0, 2, 10}};
    r.answers.push_back(a);
    auto out = serializeMessage(r);
    auto want = hex("1a2b8500000100010000000003777777076578616d706c6503636f6d0000010001"
                    "c00c0001000100000e100004c000020a");
    assert(out == want);
    puts("ok: R1 matches");

    // R2 CNAME chain
    auto r2 = hex("4d2e8500000100020000000003777777076578616d706c6503636f6d0000010001"
                  "c00c000500010000012c000603776562c010c02d000100010000012c0004c0000214");
    Message m2 = parseMessage(r2);
    assert(m2.answers.size() == 2);
    assert(DnsName::fromWire(m2.answers[0].rdata).toText() == "web.example.com.");
    assert(m2.answers[1].name.toText() == "web.example.com.");
    assert(serializeMessage(m2) == r2);
    puts("ok: R2 roundtrip");

    // R3 NXDOMAIN with SOA, R4 referral: roundtrip
    auto r3 = hex("7f018403000100000001000004 6e6f7065076578616d706c6503636f6d0000010001"
                  "c011000600010000012c0027036e7331c0110a686f73746d6173746572c011"
                  "78c3dcf100001c2000000e100012750000 00012c");
    Message m3 = parseMessage(r3);
    assert(m3.header.rcode == rcode::NXDOMAIN && m3.authority.size() == 1);
    assert(serializeMessage(m3) == r3);
    puts("ok: R3 roundtrip");
    auto r4 = hex("004280000001000000020002 03777777076578616d706c6503636f6d0000010001"
                  "c010000200010002a3000006036e7331c010"
                  "c010000200010002a3000006036e7332c010"
                  "c02d000100010002a3000004c0000235"
                  "c03f000100010002a3000004c6336435");
    Message m4 = parseMessage(r4);
    assert(serializeMessage(m4) == r4);
    puts("ok: R4 roundtrip");

    // EDNS query
    auto q2 = hex("beef01200001000000000001076578616d706c6503636f6d00001c0001 00002904d000008000 0000");
    Message m5 = parseMessage(q2);
    assert(m5.edns && m5.edns->udpSize == 1232 && m5.edns->dnssecOk && m5.header.ad);
    assert(serializeMessage(m5) == q2);
    puts("ok: Q2 EDNS roundtrip");

    // truncation: 40 A records into 512 bytes
    Message big = r;
    for (int i = 0; i < 40; ++i) big.answers.push_back(a);
    auto t = serializeMessage(big, 512);
    Message tm = parseMessage(t);
    assert(tm.header.tc && tm.answers.empty() && t.size() == 33);
    puts("ok: truncation");

    // attacks
    expectThrow(hex("000000000001000000000000c00c00010001"), "self pointer");
    expectThrow(hex("00000000000100000000000003777777c01000010001"), "forward pointer");
    expectThrow(hex("000000000001000000000000 03777777c00c00010001"), "pointer loop to own start");
    expectThrow(hex("0000000000010000000000000377"), "truncated label");
    expectThrow(hex("000000000001000000000000 4000 00010001"), "label type 01");
    expectThrow(hex("0000"), "short header");
    {
        std::vector<uint8_t> longName = hex("000000000001000000000000");
        for (int i = 0; i < 128; ++i) { longName.push_back(1); longName.push_back('a'); }
        longName.push_back(0);
        for (uint8_t b : {0, 1, 0, 1}) longName.push_back(b);
        expectThrow(longName, "128 labels (> 255 octets)");
    }

    // text names
    assert(DnsName::fromText("a\\.b.example.com.").labelCount() == 3);
    assert(DnsName::fromText("a\\.b.example.com.").toText() == "a\\.b.example.com.");
    assert(DnsName::fromText("\\065bc", DnsName::fromText("x.")).toText() == "Abc.x.");
    assert(DnsName::fromText("WWW.Example.COM.") == DnsName::fromText("www.example.com."));
    puts("all tests passed");
}
```

Output:

```text
ok: R1 matches
ok: R2 roundtrip
ok: R3 roundtrip
ok: R4 roundtrip
ok: Q2 EDNS roundtrip
ok: truncation
ok: self pointer -> compression pointer does not point backwards
ok: forward pointer -> compression pointer does not point backwards
ok: pointer loop to own start -> compression pointer does not point backwards
ok: truncated label -> label runs past end of message
ok: label type 01 -> unsupported label type
ok: short header -> unexpected end of message
ok: 128 labels (> 255 octets) -> name longer than 255 octets
all tests passed
```

### 18.5 Fuzzing

A tiny mutation fuzzer. It overwrites, truncates and inserts bytes in a referral response, and every message that parses must re-serialise into something that parses again. Build it with sanitizers:

```cpp
// Mutation fuzzer for the wire layer: random byte changes, truncations and insertions applied
// to a referral response. Every message that parses must serialise into a message that parses.
//   clang++ -std=c++20 -O1 -g -fsanitize=address,undefined -Iinclude fuzz.cpp -o fuzz && ./fuzz
#include <dns/message.hpp>

#include <cstdio>
#include <random>

using namespace dns;

int main() {
    // www.example.com A -> referral to ns1/ns2.example.com with glue (101 bytes)
    const std::vector<uint8_t> seed = {
        0x00, 0x42, 0x80, 0x00, 0x00, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x02, 0x00, 0x02, 0x03, 0x77, 0x77, 0x77,
        0x07, 0x65, 0x78, 0x61, 0x6d, 0x70, 0x6c, 0x65, 0x03, 0x63, 0x6f, 0x6d, 0x00, 0x00, 0x01, 0x00,
        0x01, 0xc0, 0x10, 0x00, 0x02, 0x00, 0x01, 0x00, 0x02, 0xa3, 0x00, 0x00, 0x06, 0x03, 0x6e, 0x73,
        0x31, 0xc0, 0x10, 0xc0, 0x10, 0x00, 0x02, 0x00, 0x01, 0x00, 0x02, 0xa3, 0x00, 0x00, 0x06, 0x03,
        0x6e, 0x73, 0x32, 0xc0, 0x10, 0xc0, 0x2d, 0x00, 0x01, 0x00, 0x01, 0x00, 0x02, 0xa3, 0x00, 0x00,
        0x04, 0xc0, 0x00, 0x02, 0x35, 0xc0, 0x3f, 0x00, 0x01, 0x00, 0x01, 0x00, 0x02, 0xa3, 0x00, 0x00,
        0x04, 0xc6, 0x33, 0x64, 0x35};
    std::mt19937 rng(1);  // fixed seed: reproducible runs
    long parsed = 0, rejected = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < 300000; ++i) {
        auto m = seed;
        for (int k = 0, muts = 1 + int(rng() % 6); k < muts; ++k) {
            switch (rng() % 3) {
            case 0: m[rng() % m.size()] = uint8_t(rng()); break;                         // overwrite a byte
            case 1: m.resize(std::max<size_t>(1, rng() % (m.size() + 1))); break;      // truncate
            case 2: m.insert(m.begin() + long(rng() % m.size()), uint8_t(rng())); break;  // insert a byte
            }
        }
        Message msg;
        try {
            msg = parseMessage(m);
        } catch (const ParseError&) {
            ++rejected;
            continue;
        }
        parseMessage(serializeMessage(msg, 512));  // must not throw: our own output is always valid
        ++parsed;
    }
    std::printf("fuzz done: %ld parsed, %ld rejected, no crashes\n", parsed, rejected);
}
```

```bash
clang++ -std=c++20 -O1 -g -fsanitize=address,undefined -Iinclude fuzz.cpp -o fuzz && ./fuzz
# fuzz done: 18454 parsed, 281546 rejected, no crashes
```

For real coverage-guided fuzzing, use libFuzzer with the same body:

```cpp
extern "C" int LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput(const uint8_t* data, size_t size) {
    try {
        auto m = dns::parseMessage({data, size});
        dns::parseMessage(dns::serializeMessage(m, 512));   // must never throw for parsed input
    } catch (const dns::ParseError&) {
    }
    return 0;
}
// clang++ -std=c++20 -g -O1 -fsanitize=fuzzer,address,undefined -Iinclude fuzz_target.cpp -o fuzz_target
// ./fuzz_target -max_total_time=30 corpus/   (seed corpus/ with the .hex vectors from §18.7, as binary)
```

A 30-second libFuzzer run of exactly this target, seeded with the 18 complete messages from this guide, executed 413,877 inputs with no crash, sanitizer report or unexpected exception. Run it for hours before you trust a parser with internet traffic.

### 18.6 Comparing against BIND, NSD, Knot and Unbound

Mature implementations are the best oracle for behaviour the RFCs leave vague.

1. **Zone validation:** `named-checkzone example.com example.com.zone` (BIND) or `kzonecheck -o example.com example.com.zone` (Knot). They should accept and reject the same files as your loader.
2. **Authoritative behaviour:** load the same zone into NSD or Knot on another port and diff the answers:

   ```bash
   for q in "www A" "web A" "nope A" "www MX" "b.c TXT" "x.wild A" "host.sub A" "x.legacy A" "example.com MX"; do
       set -- $q; n=$1; [ "$n" = example.com ] || n=$n.example.com
       diff <(dig @127.0.0.1 -p 5353 +norec +noall +answer +authority +additional "$n" "$2" | sort) \
            <(dig @127.0.0.1 -p 5354 +norec +noall +answer +authority +additional "$n" "$2" | sort) \
            > /dev/null || echo "DIFF: $n $2"
   done
   ```

   Expect differences in deliberate choices only, such as whether the zone's NS set is added to positive answers (§11.9).
3. **Resolver behaviour:** run Unbound locally and compare `dig @unbound` against `dig @yours` for a list of real names, including CNAME chains, NXDOMAINs, DNSSEC-signed names and names with glueless delegations. `unbound-control dump_cache` shows what a mature resolver caches for the same queries.
4. **Compliance tests:** the ISC "EDNS Compliance Tester" (the `genreport` tool) checks a server against RFC 8906 (EDNS versions, unknown options and flags, TCP). Run it against your server before you put it on the internet.

### 18.7 Building a test-vector set from captures

Real-world packets catch what hand-written tests miss: other implementations' compression choices, odd record types, EDNS options. To build a corpus:

1. Capture traffic: `sudo tcpdump -i any -n -s 0 -w real.pcap 'udp port 53'`. Do this only on traffic you are allowed to record. DNS queries reveal user behaviour.
2. Extract each DNS payload into a `.hex` file with the script below. It handles classic pcap with Ethernet, Linux-cooked and IPv4/IPv6 frames. It was tested on a synthetic capture that contained the §4.5 query and the §4.6 response.

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Extract DNS-over-UDP payloads from a classic pcap file into one .hex file per message.

usage: python3 pcap2vectors.py capture.pcap outdir [port]
Supports link types 1 (Ethernet, also used by `tcpdump -i lo`), 113 (Linux cooked)
and 276 (Linux cooked v2), IPv4 and IPv6, UDP only. pcapng is not supported:
convert with `editcap -F pcap in.pcapng out.pcap`.
"""
import os
import struct
import sys


def packets(path):
    with open(path, 'rb') as f:
        hdr = f.read(24)
        magic = struct.unpack('<I', hdr[:4])[0]
        if magic in (0xa1b2c3d4, 0xa1b23c4d):
            endian = '<'
        elif magic in (0xd4c3b2a1, 0x4d3cb2a1):
            endian = '>'
        else:
            raise SystemExit('not a classic pcap file (pcapng? convert with editcap -F pcap)')
        linktype = struct.unpack(endian + 'I', hdr[20:24])[0]
        while True:
            rec = f.read(16)
            if len(rec) < 16:
                return
            _, _, incl, _ = struct.unpack(endian + 'IIII', rec)
            yield linktype, f.read(incl)


def udp_payload(linktype, frame, port):
    if linktype == 1:          # Ethernet
        ethertype, off = struct.unpack('!H', frame[12:14])[0], 14
        if ethertype == 0x8100:  # one VLAN tag
            ethertype, off = struct.unpack('!H', frame[16:18])[0], 18
    elif linktype == 113:      # Linux cooked (SLL)
        ethertype, off = struct.unpack('!H', frame[14:16])[0], 16
    elif linktype == 276:      # Linux cooked v2 (SLL2)
        ethertype, off = struct.unpack('!H', frame[0:2])[0], 20
    else:
        return None
    if ethertype == 0x0800:    # IPv4
        ihl = (frame[off] & 0x0F) * 4
        if frame[off + 9] != 17 or struct.unpack('!H', frame[off + 6:off + 8])[0] & 0x3FFF:
            return None        # not UDP, or a fragment
        udp = off + ihl
    elif ethertype == 0x86DD:  # IPv6 (no extension headers)
        if frame[off + 6] != 17:
            return None
        udp = off + 40
    else:
        return None
    sport, dport, length = struct.unpack('!HHH', frame[udp:udp + 6])
    if port not in (sport, dport):
        return None
    return frame[udp + 8:udp + length]


def main():
    path, outdir = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    port = int(sys.argv[3]) if len(sys.argv) > 3 else 53
    os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)
    n = 0
    for linktype, frame in packets(path):
        payload = udp_payload(linktype, frame, port)
        if payload is None or len(payload) < 12:
            continue
        n += 1
        kind = 'resp' if payload[2] & 0x80 else 'query'
        with open(os.path.join(outdir, f'{n:05d}-{kind}.hex'), 'w') as out:
            out.write(payload.hex() + '\n')
    print(f'wrote {n} messages to {outdir}')


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

3. Feed every vector through parse → serialise → parse and compare the decoded content. Byte equality is the wrong test, because other servers compress differently.

```cpp
// Runs every *.hex test vector in a directory through parse -> serialise -> parse and checks
// that nothing is lost. Byte equality is not required: other implementations compress
// differently, so the comparison is on the decoded content.
//   clang++ -std=c++20 -Iinclude tools/check_vectors.cpp -o check_vectors && ./check_vectors vectors/
#include <dns/message.hpp>

#include <filesystem>
#include <fstream>
#include <iostream>

using namespace dns;

static bool sameRecords(const std::vector<ResourceRecord>& a, const std::vector<ResourceRecord>& b) {
    if (a.size() != b.size()) return false;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < a.size(); ++i)
        if (!(a[i].name == b[i].name) || a[i].type != b[i].type || a[i].rclass != b[i].rclass ||
            a[i].ttl != b[i].ttl || a[i].rdata != b[i].rdata)
            return false;
    return true;
}

int main(int argc, char** argv) {
    if (argc != 2) {
        std::cerr << "usage: check_vectors <dir>\n";
        return 2;
    }
    int ok = 0, bad = 0;
    for (const auto& entry : std::filesystem::directory_iterator(argv[1])) {
        if (entry.path().extension() != ".hex") continue;
        std::ifstream in(entry.path());
        std::string hex, word;
        while (in >> word) hex += word;
        std::vector<uint8_t> wire;
        for (size_t i = 0; i + 1 < hex.size(); i += 2) wire.push_back(uint8_t(std::stoul(hex.substr(i, 2), nullptr, 16)));
        try {
            Message m = parseMessage(wire);
            Message again = parseMessage(serializeMessage(m));
            bool same = again.header.flags() == m.header.flags() && again.questions.size() == m.questions.size() &&
                        sameRecords(again.answers, m.answers) && sameRecords(again.authority, m.authority) &&
                        sameRecords(again.additional, m.additional) && again.edns.has_value() == m.edns.has_value();
            if (!same) throw ParseError("round trip changed the message");
            ++ok;
        } catch (const ParseError& e) {
            std::cout << entry.path().filename().string() << ": " << e.what() << "\n";
            ++bad;
        }
    }
    std::cout << ok << " ok, " << bad << " failed\n";
    return bad ? 1 : 0;
}
```

```bash
python3 pcap2vectors.py real.pcap vectors 53
clang++ -std=c++20 -Iinclude check_vectors.cpp -o check_vectors && ./check_vectors vectors/
# with the 18 complete messages from this guide's hex dumps:  18 ok, 0 failed
```

Keep the vectors in the repository as regression tests. When a bug shows up in production, add the packet that triggered it.

## 19. Appendices

Tables 19.1–19.6 are generated from the IANA registries ("Domain Name System (DNS) Parameters", "DNS Security Algorithm Numbers", "Delegation Signer (DS) Resource Record Type Digest Algorithms", "DNS SVCB") as downloaded on 2026-10-20. Check https://www.iana.org/assignments/dns-parameters/ for later additions. Entries marked TEMPORARY in the registry are early allocations for drafts that may expire.

### 19.1 Resource record TYPEs

| Type | Value | Meaning | Reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| Reserved | 0 |  | RFC 6895 |
| A | 1 | a host address | RFC 1035 |
| NS | 2 | an authoritative name server | RFC 1035 |
| MD | 3 | a mail destination (OBSOLETE - use MX) | RFC 1035 |
| MF | 4 | a mail forwarder (OBSOLETE - use MX) | RFC 1035 |
| CNAME | 5 | the canonical name for an alias | RFC 1035 |
| SOA | 6 | marks the start of a zone of authority | RFC 1035 |
| MB | 7 | a mailbox domain name (EXPERIMENTAL) | RFC 1035 |
| MG | 8 | a mail group member (EXPERIMENTAL) | RFC 1035 |
| MR | 9 | a mail rename domain name (EXPERIMENTAL) | RFC 1035 |

### 19.7 The RFCs that matter, with one-line summaries

Status as listed by the RFC Editor in October 2026. "Obsoletes" notes are given where an older number is still widely cited.

**Core protocol**

| RFC | Title | What it gives you |
|---|---|---|
| 1034 | Domain names – concepts and facilities | the model: namespace, zones, delegation, resolver and name-server algorithms (RFC 1034 §4.3.2, §5.3.3) |
| 1035 | Domain names – implementation and specification | the wire format, RR types, compression, UDP/TCP, master files |
| 1123 | Requirements for Internet Hosts – Application and Support | host-name syntax relaxation (RFC 1123 §2.1), early resolver requirements |
| 1982 | Serial Number Arithmetic | how SOA serials (and RRSIG times) compare and wrap |
| 2181 | Clarifications to the DNS Specification | RRsets, TTL rules, trust ranking, TC bit, CNAME rules, label contents |
| 3597 | Handling of Unknown DNS RR Types | opaque RDATA, compression rules, `TYPEnnn \# …` syntax |
| 4343 | DNS Case Insensitivity Clarification | ASCII-only case folding, case preservation, `\DDD` |
| 6604 | xNAME RCODE and Status Bits Clarification | RCODE and AA in CNAME/DNAME chains |
| 6895 | DNS IANA Considerations | the registries; header and RR field allocation rules |
| 8906 | A Common Operational Problem in DNS Servers: Failure to Communicate | how to answer odd queries correctly (NOTIMP, BADVERS, unknown options) |
| 9499 | DNS Terminology (obsoletes 8499) | the vocabulary |
| 9619 | In the DNS, QDCOUNT Is (Usually) One | QDCOUNT > 1 → FORMERR |

### 19.8 Parsing and serialisation checklist

**Parsing (every message)**

- [ ] < 12 bytes → drop. Read the header field by field in network byte order.
- [ ] Server: QR=1 → drop. Opcode not implemented → NOTIMP from the header alone.
- [ ] Use counts only as loop bounds. Every read is bounds-checked against the **message** length.
- [ ] Names: label types, 63/255 limits, pointer rules (§5.6). Continue after the *first* pointer.
- [ ] RR: NAME, TYPE, CLASS, TTL, RDLENGTH ≤ remaining; RDATA parsed for known types must use exactly RDLENGTH.
- [ ] Decompress names inside RDATA of the RFC 1035 types (and leniently for the RFC 3597 §4 "SHOULD" list).
- [ ] OPT: at most one, in the additional section only, owner root; options must not overrun.
- [ ] TSIG: last record, at most one.
- [ ] Nothing left over after the last record (or a deliberate policy otherwise).
- [ ] On error after a valid header: FORMERR, with ID and opcode copied.

**Serialising (every response)**

- [ ] ID copied, QR=1, opcode copied, RD copied, CD per §15.7, question echoed **byte for byte**.
- [ ] Counts computed from what was actually written.
- [ ] Compress only owner names and RFC 1035-type RDATA names; keep offsets ≤ 0x3FFF; reset the table on rollback.
- [ ] RDLENGTH patched after writing RDATA.
- [ ] Size limit: 512 without EDNS; `min(client, own)`, at least 512, with EDNS; 65,535 on TCP.
- [ ] If an answer or authority RRset, or required glue, does not fit → TC=1 with header + question (+ OPT). Optional additional data is dropped silently.
- [ ] OPT included whenever the query had one, also in errors and truncated responses; version 0; your own payload size; unknown flags not echoed.
- [ ] Extended RCODE split correctly: header low 4 bits, OPT high 8 bits.
- [ ] TCP: one write of `length ‖ message`.

### 19.9 FAQ: common mistakes

**My responses decode as garbage in `dig`, but my parser reads them fine.**
Your parser and serialiser share the same bug. The usual suspects are host byte order (`memcpy` of a struct, missing `htons`), a wrong RDLENGTH after compression, or counts that disagree with the records actually written. Compare your bytes with the hex dumps in this guide, and open the capture in Wireshark.

**`dig` shows `ID mismatch`.**
You did not copy the query ID, or you swapped its bytes. On little-endian machines `0x1a2b` becomes `0x2b1a` if a raw `uint16_t` is copied without `htons`/`ntohs`.

**I return NXDOMAIN for names that exist only as parents of other names.**
That is an empty non-terminal. It must get NODATA (NOERROR, empty answer, SOA), as RFC 8020 requires. Insert ancestor nodes when you load the zone (§11.2).

**My wildcard answers queries for names that exist.**
A wildcard applies only when the QNAME does **not** exist, ENTs included. Check exact existence first, then the closest encloser (§11.5).

**Resolvers keep asking me for the same nonexistent name.**
Your negative answers lack the SOA in the authority section, so they cannot be cached (RFC 2308 §5). Or its TTL is 0.

**Clients hang on big answers.**
Two common causes. The response is truncated but TC is not set, or TCP is not implemented. Or you send UDP responses larger than the client's EDNS size, and they are fragmented and dropped. Cap at `min(client, 1232)` (§9.4).

**My TCP server works with `dig` but fails with real resolvers.**
Real resolvers pipeline queries, and the length prefix and message can arrive split across reads. Buffer per connection and extract complete messages in a loop (§8.4, `processTcpInput`).

**Should a CNAME and an MX record exist at the same name?**
No. CNAME excludes all other data except DNSSEC records (RFC 2181 §10.1). That is also why the zone apex can never be a CNAME. Reject such zones at load time.

**Can I compress the SRV target, the DNAME target, or names in new record types?**
No (§5.3). Only names in RFC 1035-type RDATA may be compressed. Compressing anything else corrupts data on servers that do not know the type.

**I `tolower()` names for comparison.**
`tolower()` is locale-dependent and may change bytes ≥ 0x80. Use ASCII-only folding (§3.3), and preserve the original case in responses.

**I compare names with `ends_with`.**
Then `evilexample.com` counts as "under" `example.com`. Compare whole labels (§3.6).

**I answer every query, including ones with QR=1.**
Two such servers answering each other's errors make an endless loop, and spoofing makes it a reflection attack. Drop QR=1 messages (§4.7).

**My resolver gets poisoned in tests.**
Typical causes: you accept records outside the bailiwick, let glue overwrite answers, use predictable IDs or a fixed source port, or don't check the question in the response (§12.4, §16).

**I set AA on referrals.**
Referrals are not authoritative. AA=0 (§11.7).

**I return SERVFAIL for unknown record types.**
Unknown QTYPEs get a normal answer: the RRset if you have it as opaque data, else NODATA or NXDOMAIN (RFC 3597; RFC 8906 §7).

**My EDNS responses are rejected by some resolvers.**
Check four things. You return OPT whenever the query had one. You answer version > 0 with BADVERS, not FORMERR. You ignore unknown options and flags. You never copy unknown flags back (§9.6).

**My serials stopped working after I set one too high.**
Serial comparison is modulo 2³². Use RFC 1982 arithmetic, and move forward in steps smaller than 2³¹ (§10.5).
